# Review ledger: workflow-hardening review

Transient working state for the review loop. Separate from the plan; deleted at task close. Committed so it survives context loss and travels across sessions.

Task: independently review the workflow-hardening changes (commits `8ce9454`, `1d15f22`, `8f03a9c`; diff range `f2ca59e..8f03a9c`) for correctness, quality, and consistency with the project's principles, then address valid findings.

Interrupt requests (received during this task, to fold into the plan via the planner, not done ad hoc):

- `human-onboarding`: add a "Getting started, for the human" section to the canonical `AGENTS.md` with one inline editable kickoff prompt, plus a brief README mirror pointing to it (approach B + thin C). Decided by the human.
- `ledger-template`: make the review ledger a scaffolded template asset (lean: a reference asset under `.agents/`, copied per task), so the ledger schema is pinned rather than invented ad hoc. Recommended; human leaning yes.

- `deliberation-mode`: acknowledge question-driven (Socratic) human input as a first-class entry mode; generalise the intake / clarifying-questions / Open-Questions machinery so the orchestrator answers a human question with options, trade-offs, a recommendation, and reasoning (human decides); bind each resolved question to a durable Open-Questions decision; define convergence as the human committing a decision to action, guarded by the existing "no re-raise without new evidence" rule. LOCKED by the human: approach B plus the thin C naming slice (not a new phase or role). Pairs with `human-onboarding`.
- `human-review-queue`: make the plan's "Open Questions, Decisions, Issues and Blockers" section a single living human-decision queue, each item with a stable id, a one-line ask, a status (open / decided -> folded into <step> / superseded) and a context pointer; resolved items marked resolved (not deleted) to prevent double-addressing; the orchestrator updates it at every checkpoint as a required step. Recommended (reuse Open Questions, no second artifact). Human-interface cluster with `deliberation-mode` and `human-onboarding`.

Also to fix in the planner pass: the plan's Open Questions section says "No open questions remain", which is stale (the convergence fix, the queued feature designs, and the 14 review findings are all open). Rewrite it as the living queue above.

All of these are non-trivial (touch the canonical pack assets), so they route to the planner in a single pass alongside the triager-valid review findings.

Human decisions (this checkpoint): single planner pass covering everything; `human-review-queue` = approach A (reuse the Open Questions section) upgraded with B's discipline (stable ids, explicit status, resolved-marked-not-deleted). Refinement: a new human would not know to check the queue, and a pull-only model is fragile, so the queue must be PUSH + pull: the orchestrator surfaces open items at each checkpoint, and `human-onboarding` documents the human's ongoing duty to decide on them. Planner (opus) dispatched to fold everything into the plan.

Planner returned. Plan now has six new Roadmap steps (`convergence-accounting`, `workflow-doc-fixes`, `human-onboarding`, `deliberation-mode`, `human-review-queue`, `ledger-template`), a rewritten living Open-Questions queue (Q-1..Q-10 with recommendations), and two added Success Criteria. F3 (the "not committed" contradiction) corrected in the plan's resume-anchor narrative immediately, rather than waiting for `workflow-doc-fixes`, since it would mislead a resuming agent. Next: plan review (independent reviewers on the updated plan), then the human resolves the open decisions to converge the plan, then implement. Open decisions pushed to the human: Q-1 (convergence-accounting, headline) plus Q-2..Q-7 (recommendation-backed); Q-8/Q-9/Q-10 already decided.

Human decided Q-1 = approach (b): drop the contested-rounds cap, keep a single total-round cap (default five). Marked decided in the plan queue. Q-2..Q-7 remain at their recommended defaults, awaiting confirmation. Round 2 = plan review: two independent reviewers (A plan-soundness, opus; B consistency/principles, sonnet) dispatched on the updated plan, told Q-1 = (b). Triager to follow.

NEW FINDING (found during implementation; needs a plan item): `include_dir!` in src/manifest.rs embeds pack/ but does NOT register those files as cargo rebuild dependencies, so `just scaffold-self` can regenerate the root AGENTS.md and `.agents/` from a STALE embedded pack. The bootstrap commit's committed `.agents/` copies were in fact stale (missing the hardening content); a forced `cargo clean -p agent-scaffold` + rebuild caught them up in this working tree. Impact: scaffold-self is unreliable without a forced rebuild, and the planned golden test would be fooled by the same staleness. Fix candidate: a build.rs emitting `cargo:rerun-if-changed=pack`. Route to the planner as a new step (e.g. `pack-rebuild-tracking`), prioritised before the golden test and before relying on scaffold-self. Surfaced to the human.

Human decision (triager structure): ALWAYS a separate triager, NEVER collapsed (Option 3). The triager must be independent of BOTH the producer AND the orchestrator (the orchestrator drives the loop and owns convergence/cost, so it is biased toward dismissing findings to converge), for every review round including trivial ones. Round 1: two independent reviewers dispatched (R1 correctness/termination lens, opus; R2 cross-document-consistency/principles lens, sonnet). ### Round 1 reviewer findings, pre-triage (R1: correctness / termination)

- R1-H1 (high). The recorded per-round outcome is binary (clean vs new valid findings), but the contested-rounds cap needs a third "contested" state that is never recorded or defined, and findings have no cross-round identity, so a re-spawned orchestrator cannot compute the contested cap from the ledger. (`orchestrator.md` steps 1-3; `AGENTS.md` convergence bullets and line ~136.)
- R1-H2 (high). The convergence taxonomy is not a partition: "new valid findings" and "still-contested valid findings" both describe non-clean rounds with no boundary, and the two caps use different numbers (3 vs 5) for identically recorded rounds, making the escalation trigger nondeterministic.
- R1-M1 (med). The dismissed-high-severity re-check keys on an undefined severity scale; it says "high-severity" but its stated purpose is catching a "critical" finding, which a critical-above-high scale would exclude. Severity levels are never enumerated in `reviewer.md`/`triager.md`.
- R1-M2 (med). The second-triager re-check is ordered after the round outcome is recorded, and the procedure never says what to do if the re-check overturns the dismissal (flip outcome, reset streak, refix). The blocking relationship is implied, not a step.
- R1-M3 (med). Acceptance review is described "as in the other review phases" (implying the convergence loop) but the README diagram draws it as a single pass with no loop, cap, or escalation. Prose and diagram disagree.
- R1-M4 (med). Off-by-one between prose ("exceed" / "past" / "after") and the diagram ("cap hit"); the boundary phrasings resolve to different round numbers.
- R1-M5 (med). Counts are per-artifact but the ledger is per-task; no rule resets the consecutive-clean count and round total when moving to a new artifact/step, so a re-spawned orchestrator must infer segmentation that is never specified.
- R1-M6 (med). The escalation-resume path does not reset the round counters, so a total-cap escalation can immediately re-fire on resume; the intended semantics are undefined.
- R1-L1 (low). "Risky / high-blast-radius" artifact classification is undefined yet selects the required consecutive-clean count (one vs two); subjective and not recorded.
- R1-L2 (low). The "clean round" definition ("every finding dismissed") does not explicitly cover the common zero-findings case.
- R1-L3 (low). The README diagram routes a trivial interrupt into the "implement the next step" node, a dead-end when no steps are pending, and mismatches the prose (the orchestrator folds a trivial request in directly).

### Round 1 reviewer findings, pre-triage (R2: consistency / principles, sonnet)

- R2-H1 (high). The plan doc (`docs/plans/agent-scaffold.md`, `core-assets` narrative) says the ledger is "a durable scratch file (not committed)", contradicting `AGENTS.md`, `orchestrator.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` ("committed beside its plan"). In the resume-anchor doc, so it would mislead a resuming agent into the opposite design.
- R2-M1 (med). `AGENTS.md` convergence bullets list only the contested-rounds cap; the total-round cap lives in the separate "backstops" section, so an agent scanning the bullets as a checklist gets an incomplete escalation rule.
- R2-M2 (med). Acceptance "as in the other review phases" is ambiguous about whether the convergence loop applies; the diagram shows a single pass. [= R1-M3]
- R2-M3 (med). README intake caption "human decides routing" overstates: the prose has the orchestrator fold trivial requests in directly.
- R2-M4 (med). README `intake -> impl` for trivial interrupts is wrong when the interrupt arrives during plan review (no pending step). [= R1-L3]
- R2-M5 (med). `AGENTS.md` source line-wrap splits "the review / has converged" (cosmetic; Markdown soft-wrap, to be judged).
- R2-M6 (med). `CHANGELOG.md` line-wrap leaves "guarded" at a line end (cosmetic).
- R2-L1 (low). `AGENTS.md` convergence bullet 3 says "a bounded number of rounds" not "contested rounds", ambiguous against the total cap. [related R1-M4]
- R2-L2 (low). The plan calls them "three round constants" when one (required consecutive clean rounds) has two values.

### Combined unique findings F1-F17 handed to the triager

Merged (deduped): F6 = R1-M3 + R2-M2; F7 = R1-M4 + R2-L1; F12 = R1-L3 + R2-M4. F1/F2 (deep convergence-rule defects) kept distinct from F10 (bullet omits total cap). Triager (opus) dispatched to adjudicate all 17 against the current sources.

### Round 1 triager verdicts (14 valid, 3 invalid)

Valid, to fix: F1 (high); F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, F9, F12 (medium); F8, F10, F15, F16, F17 (low). Invalid, dismissed: F11 (the diagram caption faithfully matches the prose); F13, F14 (cosmetic Markdown soft-wrap, renders as continuous text).

No high-severity finding was dismissed, so the dismissed-high-severity second-triager guard did not trigger. Round 1 outcome: NEW VALID FINDINGS (not a clean round); consecutive-clean stays 0.

Themes of the valid findings:

- Convergence accounting (F1 high, F2, F7, F10): the "contested" round state the contested-rounds cap depends on is never defined or recorded, the round taxonomy is not a partition, cap-boundary wording is inconsistent, and the bullets omit the total cap. Core defect; needs a design decision on the fix.
- Ledger/plan contradiction (F3): the plan says the ledger is "not committed" vs the prompts/CHANGELOG "committed beside its plan".
- Under-specified mechanics (F5 re-check overturn handling, F8 counter reset across artifacts, F9 escalation-resume counter reset, F15 risk classification, F16 zero-findings clean round).
- Severity scale undefined (F4): guard triggers on "high" but aims at "critical".
- Acceptance loop ambiguity (F6): "as in other phases" vs single-pass diagram.
- Diagram vs prose (F12): trivial-interrupt edge routes to impl, a dead-end pre-implementation.
- Nit (F17): "three round constants" imprecise (one has two values).

Next: single planner pass folding the 14 valid findings plus the three queued feature requests into the plan; then plan review, implement, round 2 review.

## Post-189b7bd notes (re-added; an implementer's fmt + git checkout clobbered these)

INCIDENT / lesson: the `workflow-doc-fixes` implementer ran repo-wide `just fmt` (which reformats this ledger and the plan as a side effect), then reverted the ledger with `git checkout docs/plans/agent-scaffold.ledger.md` to honour "do not touch the ledger". That reverted the ledger to HEAD (189b7bd), discarding the uncommitted Q-14 notes. Mitigations going forward: (a) commit the ledger before spawning an implementer, so its uncommitted state is not at risk; (b) implementers must NOT run repo-wide `just fmt` or `git checkout` on files they do not own (fmt only their files; leave incidental reformatting for the orchestrator). A real workflow finding (candidate plan item); reinforces committing the ledger frequently and the structured-record / findings-files work.

Q-14 (findings-to-files) DECIDED by the human: adopt. Reviewers and triagers write their findings to per-agent files that other agents read directly (parallel-safe); the ledger references them by path and keeps the decision trail; an orchestrator-owned cleanup step deletes them once the round is fully resolved or at task close. Compose with `ledger-template` (schema) and `state-schema`, one schema and validator. Reasoning: Principle 5 (removes the lossy transcription hop that dropped A-M5) and Principle 1 (findings file = single source, referenced not copied). Fold into the structured-record cluster at the next planner touch; non-blocking.

`workflow-doc-fixes` implemented (F4/F5/F6/F8/F9/F12/F15/F16/F17); regenerated, 46 tests/clippy/fmt clean, ASCII-clean; not committed. Reviewing it with TWO reviewers (A correctness/completeness, opus; B consistency/principles, sonnet), DOGFOODING Q-14: each reviewer WRITES its findings to a file under `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.reviews/`, and the separate triager reads those files directly (no orchestrator transcription). Files cleaned up at round close.

New human request (interrupt): queue the `optional-modules` agent-isolation work (agent-images / agent-box) to prevent the cross-agent file-conflict class (the git-checkout ledger clobber). Intake (per the human-input contract). Recommendation: DECOUPLE the incident from the isolation feature. (1) Now, fix the incident cheaply with discipline (implementers format only their own files, never `git checkout` files they do not own; the orchestrator commits the ledger before spawning writers) as a small workflow-rule plan item, sibling to `pack-rebuild-tracking`. (2) Pursue isolation deliberately for its real value (parallel implementation, sandboxing), NOT as the incident fix; when taken up, do WORKTREE isolation first (native to the harness, proportionate to the file-conflict class) and the heavier `agent-box` / `agent-images` CONTAINER isolation as a later follow-on (adds sandboxing/environment isolation, a separate motivation). Reasoning: Principle 2 (minimal; do not over-build the core for one incident), Principle 6 (evidence before heavy investment), Principle 1 (isolation is the cleaner structural answer, so pursue it, but deliberately and worktree-first). Awaiting the human's decision on queuing and priority.

`workflow-doc-fixes` review (Q-14 findings files, no orchestrator transcription):

- Reviewer A (correctness): 0/0/0/3L -> `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.reviews/workflow-doc-fixes-A.md`.
- Reviewer B (consistency): 0/1H/2M/3L -> `.../workflow-doc-fixes-B.md`. The high: `orchestrator.md` omits "acceptance does not require clean rounds" (F6 drift vs `AGENTS.md`). Theme: the F6/F15/F5 clauses landed in `AGENTS.md` but were not mirrored into `orchestrator.md` (the operational prompt), plus backstop-guard wording drifts across AGENTS.md/orchestrator.md/triager.md. Separate triager (opus, independent of the orchestrator and the implementer) dispatched to adjudicate; it reads BOTH findings files directly (Q-14).

Agent-isolation decision (human, refining the earlier intake): adopt a CAPABILITY-TIERED, harness-agnostic rule. Run each WRITER agent in the strongest isolation the harness supports, preference order: (1) container isolation (agent-box / agent-images) if available, PREFERRED (isolates filesystem + environment + security sandbox); (2) worktree isolation if that is what is available (e.g. claude-code native); (3) discipline-only fallback (scoped fmt, no cross-owner git checkout, commit the ledger before writers) when the harness has no isolation. Read-only agents (reviewers reading, triager) need no isolation (Principle 2). The discipline fix is the always-on BASELINE, not replaced; isolation is the structural upgrade layered on when available. "Container-preferred" is the runtime selection rule (in the workflow docs); our own build/adoption order stays worktree-first (native to claude-code, cheap now), agent-box container integration later; the rule resolves to worktree for us today anyway. Fold into `optional-modules` (isolation) plus a small discipline workflow-rule item at the next planner touch.

Fix = mirror the clauses into `orchestrator.md` + the two README nits. Committing the ledger (protect it), then resuming the implementer WITH the discipline rule (no repo-wide fmt, no git checkout). Review files kept until the step converges, then cleaned up (Q-14).

MISTAKE: I deleted the Q-14 review files with `rm -rf` while they were UNTRACKED (never committed), so their raw content is not recoverable from git; the findings survive only in summary here and in the triager verdicts. Exactly the case the human's new rule prevents.

New human rule (DECIDED): COMMIT BEFORE DELETING any workflow-managed file (findings files, the ledger at task close, any transient artifact), so the deletion is a committed deletion recoverable from git history. Resolves the Q-14 committed-vs-transient sub-decision toward COMMITTED (a findings file is committed at least once, then removed as a committed deletion). Fold into Q-14's cleanup step, the ledger delete-at-close lifecycle, and a general workflow rule. Applies from now.

`pack-rebuild-tracking` implemented (build.rs, std-only, recursive `cargo:rerun-if-changed` for pack/). Validated by the orchestrator: 46 tests pass; a pack edit re-embeds on a plain `just build` with NO `cargo clean` (marker reached the scaffolded output, then reverted; tree clean). The implementer was accidentally killed by the human mid-task but had already reverted its temp edit, so recovery was just: inspect git status (only `?? build.rs`), re-validate.

Robustness-to-mishaps decision (human asked; per the human-input contract): a killed or interrupted agent can leave the tree inconsistent. Adopt LAYERED robustness: (baseline, now) a clean-tree-before-writer invariant (commit pending work before spawning a writer, so a kill's damage is a visible uncommitted diff); an orchestrator RECOVERY PROTOCOL on any kill/interrupt (inspect git status/diff, revert stray temp artifacts, discard or complete partial work, confirm a known-good tree before continuing); and validation-in-scratch (destructive validations run in a temp dir or worktree, not the live tree). (structural, later) ISOLATION (already decided) is the real fix, a killed isolated writer cannot touch the main tree; this is the SECOND mishap (after the fmt/checkout clobber) that is evidence to prioritise it. Unifying theme: git is the workflow's durability/recovery substrate. Fold into the discipline/isolation cluster at the next planner touch.

Robustness-to-mishaps DECIDED by the human: adopt the layered robustness, baseline now (clean-tree-before-writer invariant, orchestrator recovery protocol on any kill/interrupt, validation-in-scratch) and isolation as the structural fix (already decided; this mishap is added evidence to prioritise it). Fold into the discipline/isolation cluster at the next planner touch.

Gate-prompt finding (`Q-20`, from a human question, transcript-recovered). The human asked whether `pack/prompts/clarifying-questions.md` and `open-questions-gate.md` are for humans. A compaction had flattened their real nature (agent-RUN, human-ANSWERED decision GATES: the "me" in them is the human) into "agent-facing role prompts", so the orchestrator first answered wrongly ("not for humans"). Combed the transcript (jq/ripgrep over the 48MB session file): design intent is at [07-11T10:17] ("answer at the gates: clarifying questions before work starts ... you're the decider there") and the same discussion is where the kickoff prompt was born; git shows the files came from `a135ff9`, and `704bbae` made open-questions-gate principle-agnostic after an earlier human worry. Relationship: `kickoff.md` (human-invoked) is the ENTRANCE (human -> workflow); the gates are where the running workflow comes BACK to the human (workflow -> human); `human-onboarding`'s "Getting started" section documents both ends. DECIDED (human): keep the gates as the planner's prompts (do not fold into `planner.md`), fix the "ask me" routing (sub-agent -> orchestrator -> human), and correct the README's "one prompt per role" mislabel. New step `gate-prompt-clarity` inserted before `compaction-prep`. Recording the plan + this finding, then implementing + reviewing.

`gate-prompt-clarity` implemented (commit 41cf5ca; before fc30bb3): reworded both gate prompts to name the human as decider + state sub-agent -> orchestrator -> human routing; fixed the README `prompts/` "one prompt per workflow role" mislabel. LOW-risk -> one clean round.

No high/critical -> no backstop. Round 1 outcome: NEW VALID. Between-step checkpoint finding (`Q-21`, OPEN, from a human question). At a step boundary the orchestrator paused with an ad-hoc "carry on or pause?" that was neither a defined human-input point nor in the human-input-contract format. Two causes: (1) an operator miss, the human-input contract (`Q-12`, still not in the pack until `deliberation-mode` lands) was not self-applied, so the decision was a thin binary instead of options/trade-offs/recommendation/reasoning; (2) a real design gap, the workflow has the orchestrator drive step-to-step autonomously and defines no between-step checkpoint. Recorded `Q-21` (open) for the human to decide the cadence (autonomous / report-only / gate / configurable), recommendation (d)+(b). The FORMAT half is `deliberation-mode`'s job. Human chose to proceed with `deliberation-mode` now; `Q-21` stays open in the queue for the human's decision.

So that finding is dismissed (not a defect); reverted the pointless reflow; recorded the preference in memory (`no-hard-wrap-prose`) and `Q-22`. `no-wrap-convention` implemented (`Q-22`). Part 1 (convention, implementer, commit `4fa5e74`): added a "Prose formatting" paragraph to `pack/AGENTS.md` (prose is not hard-wrapped; line length is never a review finding) and the line-length-never-a-finding rule to `pack/prompts/reviewer.md` and `pack/prompts/triager.md`; added a repo-root `.prettierrc.json` `{ "proseWrap": "never" }`. Part 2 (formatter, orchestrator): a pre-reflow safety fix reworded a ledger line so prettier would not read a literal `>` as a blockquote (`81aa4bd`); then a one-time repo-wide `nix fmt` reflow unwrapped all markdown prose (`dc5f69b`, 29 files, +554/-2530), and the `scaffold-self` recipe now runs `nix fmt` after the raw render so it reaches a stable fixed point. Diagnosed and fixed a treefmt CACHE artifact: files unchanged since before `.prettierrc.json` were stale-cached as clean (from the old `proseWrap: preserve`) and skipped, so a one-time `nix fmt -- --no-cache` was needed to reflow the untouched pack prompts; a fresh checkout has an empty cache, so this is an in-session issue, not a committed defect. Verified: `nix fmt` and `scaffold-self` are idempotent (stable git tree), 46 tests + clippy pass, all markdown ASCII-clean, code fences balanced, the plan Roadmap and ledger tables intact, the plan was already unwrapped (untouched). Classified RISKY / high-blast-radius (repo-wide reflow of shipped pack prompts + a change to the formatter contract and the dogfooding recipe) -> TWO clean rounds required. Round 1: two independent reviewers dispatched (opus reflow-integrity, sonnet convention/tooling-correctness; findings to Q-14 files), separate triager to follow.

`no-wrap-convention` round 1 findings: opus (reflow-integrity) CLEAN, 0 findings (word-diff shows only the three intentional convention additions; structural marker counts identical; generated copies match pack sources). sonnet (convention/tooling) 2 low: F1 = the `scaffold-self` `nix fmt` formats the whole repo, not just generated files (done-but-not-decided, comment misleading); F2 = the justfile behavioural change landed in a `style:` commit. Separate triager (opus, read both files per Q-14, `no-wrap-convention-triage.md`): both VALID low; opus CLEAN verdict independently upheld. F1 fix: KEEP the whole-repo `nix fmt` (scoping would duplicate the manifest dest list against Principle 1), correct the misleading recipe comment, and add a plan note that whole-repo formatting is intentional (treefmt idempotent). F2: ACCEPT the residual risk (do not rewrite unpushed local history; an accepted risk does not block convergence). No high/critical raised or dismissed, so no backstop re-check. Round 1 outcome: NEW VALID FINDINGS (F1); RISKY artifact, streak 0 of 2. Applied the F1 fix (justfile comment + plan note) and recorded F2 acceptance, then round 2 (fresh reviewer); two consecutive clean rounds still required to converge.

Zero findings. ORCHESTRATOR CONVERGENCE CORRECTION: the reviewer asserted this was "the second consecutive clean round" and that convergence was met, but that is wrong and convergence accounting is the orchestrator's, not the reviewer's. Round 1 had a valid finding (F1), so it was NOT clean; round 2 is the FIRST clean round. Streak = 1 of 2 for this RISKY artifact. STEP 93 `checks-runner-worktree-name-collision` IS A NEW DEFERRED STEP (order 93, `low_risk`, needs 1 clean round), branch `plan/test-isolation` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/plan-test-isolation`), UNMERGED. It records a REAL test-suite defect found incidentally by a step-92 triager: `src/checks.rs` names checker worktrees `{pid}-{nanos}`, `nanos()` is a bare `SystemTime::now()` read with no uniqueness guarantee, and cargo runs a crate's tests as THREADS OF ONE PROCESS so the pid is shared, letting two concurrent `run()` calls take over each other's worktree. Observed once (one `cargo test` failed two `checks::tests`, five later runs green); this supplies the mechanism for the previously unexplained flake the ledger recorded during the audit work. Production is SAFE (separate CLI invocations are separate processes); the SUITE is not deterministically green. Round records 217 (r1, 5 valid), 219 (r2, 3 valid), 220 (r3, 2 valid), r4 record NOT YET WRITTEN.

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT (4), recorded like the other three so it is not repeated: THE ORCHESTRATOR ASSERTED A FALSE FACT TO THE HUMAN INSIDE A RECOMMENDATION. It told the human this would be "the project's FIRST waiver ever" and offered "gives W5's integrity join its first test on live data" as a REASON to choose it. Both are false: the plan already carries NINETEEN `[[step.waiver]]` entries, FOUR of them `accepted-at-escalation` + `record-backed`, the exact kind being written. The cause was a bad check, not a bad memory: it grepped the JSONL (which legitimately has zero, that substrate being the legacy form) and the TOML pattern `waiver = \[`, which matches ONLY the empty inline arrays and never the populated `[[step.waiver]]` table syntax. It then reported the empty result as an established fact rather than as a check that might not have matched what it was looking for. THE LESSON: a grep returning zero is evidence about the PATTERN as much as about the repo, and a zero result used as a load-bearing premise must be confirmed by a second pattern that is known to match something. The decision was unaffected (the human's choice is better supported with four precedents than with none) and the correction was given immediately, but the human was briefly reasoning from a false premise the orchestrator supplied.

ALSO WORTH THE SUBSTRATE NOTE: the orchestrator first wrote the waiver as a JSONL `type:"waiver"` record and W3 did NOT clear, because with `[meta].primary = "toml"` the waivers are read by `waivers_from_toml` (`src/workflow.rs:192`) and the JSONL `parse_waivers` path (`:165`) backs only the Markdown-sourced flow. The JSONL record was therefore INERT, and was removed rather than left as a second, unread copy of the same fact (One source of truth). This is the same TOML-supersedes-JSONL shape as `[meta].w4_baseline` versus the `type:"baseline"` record.

CALIBRATION, ANSWERING A HUMAN QUESTION ASKED AT THE CLOSE ("why six rounds, is that normal"): NO, it is an outlier. Across all 77 artifacts ever reviewed in this log the distribution is 1 round x16, 2 x35, 3 x10, 4 x6, 5 x6, 6 x2, 7 x1, 9 x1, so the MEDIAN IS 2 and six puts step 92 joint-third of 77, level with `structured-skeleton` and behind only `driver-output-generation` (7) and `workflow-driver` (9). Those are large structural steps; step 92 is one test file's comment block. Project-wide severity mix for context: 301 low, 76 medium, 12 high, 1 critical. Step 92's distinction is that ALL 15 of its findings were low prose, with zero mechanism defects. THE DIAGNOSED CAUSE: the block's subject is "what does this guard cover", and a coverage claim is unusually easy to falsify at an edge, so each fresh lens found another true-sounding sentence with a false boundary; compounding that, THREE OF THE SIX ROUNDS WERE SELF-INFLICTED, since every fix pass that AUTHORED prose (rounds 1, 2, 4) manufactured the next round's finding while all three deletion-class passes (round 3, and both escalation fixes) re-seeded nothing. This is now the third independent confirmation of that pattern in this task and it should change future practice: CONSTRAIN FIX PASSES TO DELETIONS WHERE THE DEFECT ALLOWS IT, and give the implementer an explicit report-do-not-fix escape valve, which was used correctly on all three of this step's escalation passes.

`F1-COLDREAD` (`medium`, VALID, FIX REQUIRED) IS THE ROUND'S REAL FINDING, and it is the one the fidelity lens structurally could not catch, because the change was faithful to a brief that was itself insufficient here. At `pack/prompts/orchestrator.md:31` the pre-existing second-person imperative "record the resolved answer as a durable Open-Questions decision" SURVIVED and the routing clause was appended BESIDE it, so the same sentence COMMANDS an act and ROUTES IT AWAY. Because the Open Questions queue IS `[[question]]` entries in the plan TOML, and because the Socratic path has no create-then-decide stage, recording a decided Socratic answer MINTS a `[[question]]`, which is exactly what `Q-67` reserves for the planner. An orchestrator can read "recording" and "the fold" as separate acts, author the `[[question]]` itself, and route only downstream `[[step]]` work, re-permitting the precise misstep this step exists to prevent.

THE TRIAGER REFUSED THE IMPLEMENTER'S COUNTER-ARGUMENT ON EVIDENCE, and the reasoning is worth keeping because both grounds looked plausible. The implementer had deliberately kept the verb, arguing that deleting it would move EVERY fold off the orchestrator including the trivial ones, and that it was matching the structure of `pack/AGENTS.md:43`. Ground one is a FALSE PREMISE: the scope constraint forbids an UNQUALIFIED "the planner authors that fold", so re-voicing the verb while keeping "non-trivial (authoring a `[[question]]` or a `[[step]]`)" verbatim moves no trivial fold anywhere; the implementer conflated "keep the imperative" with "keep trivial folds with the orchestrator", which are two separate choices. Ground two is FALSE AT THE DISPUTED POINT: `pack/AGENTS.md:43` uses three ACTOR-LESS verbs ("becomes", "routed", "edited in") while the prompt kept "record" as a SECOND-PERSON IMPERATIVE. The decisive evidence is structural: the Socratic mode at `:43` has NO create-then-decide stage, whereas the exploration mode at `:45` explicitly does ("records the question ... with status `exploring` ... only then does it move the item to `open`"); and `src/plan/source.rs:314-317` requires `folded_into` when a question is `decided`, so a Socratic recording mints a `[[question]]` AND must name a `[[step]]`. The step's own brief had also forbidden this outcome in advance at its lines 8 and 20. EVERY CITATION IN BOTH FINDINGS FILES REPRODUCED, with no misnumbering for the first time in several rounds.

ROUND 3 VERDICTS. `AD1` was SPLIT by the triager because the halves differ in consequence and in fix cost by an order of magnitude. `AD1a` (`medium`, VALID, fix required): the reservation's claim ERROR arm is executed by no test. The triager confirmed the reviewer's two mutations (`unwrap_or(false)` and bare `?`, both GREEN at 385) but RE-GROUNDED the severity on a mutation NEITHER LENS TRIED, `claim(&path).unwrap_or(true)`, also GREEN at 385, under which the reservation returns a path whose claim was never established, falsifying `src/checks.rs:483-485` ("that outcome, not an entropy argument, is what makes the returned path exclusively ours"). The reviewer's own stated ground, message quality, would have carried only `low`. This is the same class as round 2's `X2` and is NOT covered by it: `X2` pinned a LOST claim and says nothing about an ERRORED one, and round 2's table recorded this arm as not tried. `AD1b` (`low`, VALID BUT ACCEPT RESIDUAL): the temp-dir creation error arm degrades by one noun while both messages still name the path and the same errno and exit 2; pinning it costs a whole new spawned-binary integration test because `std::env::temp_dir()` has no seam, and injecting the temp dir is the second-testability-parameter move round 2 already declined for the clock. `AD2` (`low`, VALID, fix required, FRAMING CORRECTED): `message.contains(&RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS.to_string())` can be satisfied by digits from the path itself. The reviewer's 100/100 spurious-pass figure DID NOT REPRODUCE; the triager re-measured 10 PASS / 90 FAIL over 100 fresh processes and found the digits arriving from the 19-digit nanos rather than the pid, so the assertion does not pin nothing, it pins at a rate that is a property of the developer's pid counter and clock digits, 0 percent on the reviewer's machine and 87 to 90 percent on the triager's. `AD3` (`low`, VALID, fix required, REMEDY CHANGED TO DELETION): the comment written to fix round 2's `X8b` states in the present tense that nothing exercises the lost-claim verdict, the retry, or the exhaustion error and that each can be deleted with a green suite; all three measured false against the tree the comment sits in.

THE MINIMAL FIX SET, all three built, measured and reverted by the triager: 386 passed, clippy 0/0, TEN consecutive full `cargo test` runs green, +31/-4 in `src/checks.rs`, and ZERO PRODUCTION CODE CHANGED. Fix A (`AD1a`): one 27-line unit test on the EXISTING seam, RED under all three claim-error mutations. Fix B (`AD2`): one line, `contains(&RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS.to_string())` -> `contains(&format!("after {RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS} attempts"))`, which moves the measured kill rate from 10/100 to 100/100 while keeping the symbolic constant so the settled `16 -> 3` behaviour is unchanged. Fix C (`AD3`): DELETE the false clause, net -2 lines, ZERO words authored.

PRESCRIPTION TESTED NOT TRUSTED, FOURTH INSTANCE, and this one cut in three directions rather than the usual one. The `AD1` fix was advertised by its author as two tests, 77 lines, 387 passing; BUILT, it is ONE test, 27 lines, 386 passing, and it kills a mutation the reviewer never had, with the reviewer's second test measured as disproportionate and declined. `AD2`'s proposal was RIGHT IN SUBSTANCE but was unbuilt by its author and its headline measurement did not reproduce. `AD3`'s diagnosis was right and its METHOD was wrong: the reviewer prescribed a past-tense rewrite, which the triager declined explicitly as re-seeding, choosing deletion instead. The running tally is now `FN-2` (step 92), round 1's `T1`, round 2's clock parameter, and all three of round 3's.

ALL FOUR ROUND-1 FIXES LANDED, each verified by mutation rather than by reading. Neither lens could break the uniqueness property, and neither could make the prune reclaim a LIVE worktree.

THE ROUND'S THROUGH-LINE, MEASURED BY THE TRIAGER AND STRONGER THAN EITHER LENS PUT IT: FOUR OF FIVE LOAD-BEARING PARTS CAN EACH BE DELETED ALONE WITH 382 TESTS GREEN AND CLIPPY SILENT, namely the `create_dir_all(&temp)`, the atomic sequence, the `if claimed` verdict, and the retry loop. The suite goes red only when the whole construction goes. TWO RECORDED EXPLANATIONS ARE THEREFORE FALSE AND ARE CORRECTED HERE: the implementer's "the 16 retries absorb the collisions" and round 1's "the retry path carries the property". Instrumentation shows deepest retry depth 1 and 2020 attempts for 2000 reservations WITH AND WITHOUT the layer-1 mutation, so there are ZERO collisions and ZERO retries; with layer 1 disabled AND a panic planted on the lost-claim branch, `checks::` passes 30/30 three times. The property test's redness is carried by the `mkdir` syscall's CLOCK SPACING, not by either layer, and the margin is thin (1 to 15 duplicates per 2000, red 8 of 8).

A THIRD UNSOUND REVIEWER PRESCRIPTION, CAUGHT THE SAME WAY. The verification lens proposed a clock parameter plus a frozen-clock test and claimed it would also close the retry hole. The triager BUILT IT AND MEASURED: green on committed code, RED under `fetch_add` -> `load`, but GREEN under `RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS = 1` and GREEN under `if claimed || true`, so its advertised side-benefit is FALSE, because on correct code a frozen clock never loses a claim. The adversarial lens's injectable-claim prescription does work as described and was adopted instead. THAT IS THREE FOR THREE IN THIS TASK: `FN-2` (step 92), the round-1 `T1` proposal, and now this. THE STANDING LESSON, now beyond doubt: A REVIEWER'S DIAGNOSIS AND ITS PRESCRIPTION CARRY DIFFERENT WEIGHT, and a prescription must be executed and measured before it is trusted.

ROUND 2 VERDICTS. `X2` (`medium`, VALID, = adversarial F2 merged with the retry half of verification F1): the loop's collision handling is executed by NOTHING; `if claimed` -> `if claimed || true` at `:524` leaves the full suite, the new exclusivity test AND the property test all green. This is the unclosed remainder of round 1's `T1`. `X3` (`medium`, VALID): the round-1 `TMPDIR` regression fix is pinned by no test; deleting `:501-506` leaves the suite green while `TMPDIR=<missing>` goes exit 0 -> exit 2. `X4` (`medium`, VALID for its PROSE half only): the prune permanently SKIPS A REGISTERED DEAD ORPHAN when git records a symlink-resolved path outside `std::env::temp_dir()`, and both prune tests FAIL under a symlinked `TMPDIR`; the MECHANISM IS PRE-EXISTING (prune body byte-identical `HEAD~2..HEAD`) but the Invariant B text THIS step newly wrote states the bound as registration only, which is incomplete. `X1` (`low`, ACCEPT RESIDUAL WITH THE REASON CORRECTED): layer 1 is a NAME OPTIMISATION and correctness rides on layer 2, which fix 2 pins end to end; the previously recorded reason was wrong and must not be repeated. `X5`, `X6` (`low`, ACCEPT RESIDUAL), `X7` (`low`, record correction only: the commit message quotes an errno the binary does not produce), `X8` (`low`, VALID, two comments this commit wrote are false).

STEP 93 WORK-REVIEW ROUND 1 (2026-07-30, record 228, outcome `new_valid`, 12 raw findings deduplicated to 10, 8 VALID and 2 DISMISSED, severities 3 `medium` + 5 `low`; NO high or critical raised or dismissed so NO backstop owed; streak 0 of the 2 its `risky` class needs). Implementer commit `b890c4a`, `src/checks.rs` only, +212/-26. IT CHOSE (a)+(d) AND MEASURED THE CHOICE rather than arguing it: an 8-process probe at the constant-pid fixture template gave (a)-alone duplicates in 8 of 8 runs and (a)+(d) zero, with `create_dir` actually rejecting a name twice in 80000, so layer 2 is not decorative. THE UNIQUENESS PROPERTY ITSELF HOLDS and both lenses verified it independently; the fix set below is about COVERAGE AND EDGES, not about the fix being wrong.

THE RED-3 MUTATION VINDICATED THE `risky` CLASSIFICATION. Prepending the disambiguator instead of appending it (`{seq}-{pid}-{nanos}`) breaks `owning_pid` parsing and makes the startup prune RECLAIM A LIVE WORKTREE, confirmed independently by the implementer, the evidence lens and the triager. The evidence lens found it STRONGER than reported: run in isolation a third prune test also fails, so the implementer's "one prune test still passed" was an artifact of which sequence values a run draws, not a property. That is precisely the destructive failure the classification was made for, and the fix now carries a named test pinning the pid's position.

BOTH LENSES INDEPENDENTLY FOUND THE SAME CENTRAL DEFECT, which is the strongest signal this round produced: NEITHER LAYER OF THE FIX IS PINNED BY A TEST (`T1`, `medium`, RAISED from the evidence lens's `low`). All four mutations reproduce: `create_dir` -> `create_dir_all` DESTROYS EXCLUSIVITY yet the whole suite stays green; the `AlreadyExists` retry arm is NEVER EXECUTED ANYWHERE, proved by panic-probing it and staying green; `fetch_add` -> `load` (a constant sequence) stays green 3 of 3; and deleting the reservation is caught only INCIDENTALLY by a cleanup `.unwrap()` whose message says nothing about reservations. So the suite pinned "at least one disambiguator survives", not "both layers do", in a step whose entire purpose is establishing those properties.

THE TRIAGER DISPROVED THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEWER'S OWN PRESCRIBED FIX, BY IMPLEMENTING IT. The reviewer proposed a 3-line test (assert the reserved path `is_dir()`, then a second `fs::create_dir` returns `AlreadyExists`) and claimed it would kill three mutations. The triager wrote that exact test and measured: it passes on clean code, and UNDER THE `create_dir_all` MUTATION THE WHOLE `checks::` MODULE IS STILL GREEN (30 passed, 0 failed), because it tests `std` rather than the production site and never drives the retry arm. Implementing it as written would have produced a GREEN ROUND THAT PINS NOTHING. This is the SECOND time in this task that a reviewer's proposed remedy was unsound and the triager caught it by MEASURING rather than reasoning; the first was step 92's `FN-2`. RECORD THE LESSON: a reviewer's diagnosis and a reviewer's prescription carry different weight, and the prescription must be executed and measured before it is trusted.

THE AUTHORISED FIX SET, four fixes all in `src/checks.rs`, verified single-site by the triager. (1) `T1` (`medium`): extract `claim_dir(path) -> io::Result<bool>` (7 lines, called from the reservation loop) plus a 4-line exclusivity test; the triager MEASURED this green on `b890c4a`, RED under `create_dir_all`, and RED under a panic in the taken branch, so it kills the mutations the reviewer's version left alive. (2) `T2` (`medium`, a REAL BEHAVIOURAL REGRESSION the evidence lens missed): `fs::create_dir` rather than `create_dir_all` turns a `TMPDIR` naming a missing directory from WORKING into a HARD FAILURE, because `git worktree add` used to create the leading directories, and the error lost all context (`No such file or directory (os error 2)` against the pre-fix `WorktreeSetup` message naming the operation and full path); fix by `create_dir_all(&temp)` before the loop with path-carrying error context, and correct the comment claiming the reservation "changes nothing downstream", which is false. Fixes 1 and 2 land in one edit. (3) `T3` (`medium`): Module Invariant B at `:37-41` and two comments restating it at `:324-326` and `:816-817` are NOW FALSE, because a SIGKILL between `create_dir` and `worktree add` leaves an UNREGISTERED directory the prune cannot see; qualify all three, and do NOT widen the prune to sweep the temp dir, which would give it authority over other repositories' runner directories. (4) `T6` (`low`): move two `fs::remove_dir` calls above the assertion loop so a failing fixture does not leak. ACCEPTED RESIDUALS, do not re-raise: `T4` (a bypass at the production call site stays green; the brief already settled that the grep is the proportional evidence), `T5` (the `WorktreeGuard`-before-add move is unpinned; pinning it needs failure injection into `run()`, disproportionate), `T7`, `T10`. DISMISSED: `T8`, `T9`. ONE OFF-BY-ONE citation was found in a findings file (`:439-440` where the sentence is at `:440-441`), not a dismissal ground.

HUMAN DECIDED (2026-07-30) where the three `agent-scaffold next` defects go: FOLD THEM INTO GATE 4, the validation-constraints step, rather than giving them their own step, patching only the missing increment, or deferring them past the rename. Ground: the false-completion bug is a false green of the same family as `Q-55`, and its fix IS a constraint (a step's declared `[[step.increment]]` set should cover what its own brief commits to, and `next` must not assert completion from a converged last increment alone), so one planner pass covers both and the rename gates stay at three. This is a scheduling sub-decision, so no `[[question]]` and no receipt.

STEP 93 `checks-runner-worktree-name-collision` WORK LOOP OPENED (2026-07-30), GATE 1 OF 3 FOR THE RENAME. RISK CLASS = `risky`, so 2 consecutive clean rounds converge. CLASSIFIED BY THE ORCHESTRATOR AT LOOP-OPEN per its duty under the convergence rule, and stated to the human rather than put to them, because the rule assigns this call to the orchestrator. GROUNDS, and note they cut AGAINST the "this project over-classes prose work" calibration recorded for step 90: this is CODE, not prose, and the failure mode runs through `WorktreeGuard`'s `remove_dir_all` on a shared path (`src/checks.rs:329-342`) plus a startup prune that decides what to reclaim by parsing the owning pid out of the name (`owning_pid`, `src/checks.rs:400-405`). A wrong name format can therefore make the prune RECLAIM A LIVE WORKTREE, which is destructive and not cheaply reversible, and that is the definition of risky the rule gives. The artifact is EXACTLY: `src/checks.rs` (the shared name generator, the `run()` call site, the three fixtures at `:1462`/`:1491`/`:1492`, and the four comments the brief names at `:72-77`, `:400-402`, `:789-790`, `:845-847`) plus the new property test. If it grows beyond that, the classification gets revisited EXPLICITLY. THE STEP NEEDS AN IMPLEMENTER, NOT A PLANNER: its plan review already converged over 4 rounds. THE ONE GENUINE FORK the brief leaves open is which candidate fix to take, (a) an atomic counter, (d) a `create_dir` reservation, or the (a)+(d) composition; the brief instructs the implementer to pick and ARGUE it, or to raise it if it is a real fork, so it is deliberately NOT pre-decided here and will come to the human only if the implementer raises it.

THREE DEFECTS IN `agent-scaffold next`, FOUND BY ACTUALLY RUNNING IT (2026-07-30), and the orchestrator's own dogfooding gap that hid them. THE GAP FIRST: the orchestrator had been reading the plan TOML directly with grep and awk and had NOT been running `next` at all, so none of this was visible until the human asked whether the tool was still being dogfooded. Running the tool you are building is not optional; reading its source data by hand routes around exactly the surface a user depends on. (1) `next` RECOMMENDS A FALSE COMPLETION, the most serious of the three. It reports `workflow-driver / workflow-driver-stage1 ... state: converged, streak: 2/2 -> mark-step-complete` with the advice "mark the step complete, re-render, and commit". That advice is WRONG: `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.steps/workflow-driver.md:15` states "The committed near-term path is 0a -> 0b -> 1 -> 2", and `:20` describes Stage 2 (the typed `src/driver/` FSM engine) as committed near-term work that is NOT BUILT. The step is correctly `in-progress`. The mechanism of the defect: `next` infers completion from THE LAST DECLARED INCREMENT CONVERGING, and the plan declares only `stage0a`, `stage0b`, `stage1` as `[[step.increment]]` entries while Stage 2 is committed ONLY IN THE SIDECAR PROSE, with no structured entry. So the structured source is INCOMPLETE relative to the prose, and `next` reads the structured source and concludes the work is done. Had the orchestrator followed the advice it would have marked a step complete over committed unbuilt work, which is a FALSE GREEN of exactly the class the rename is being gated on, emitted by the tool's own advisory surface. (2) `next` OUTPUT IS 79503 BYTES, because it dumps the ledger RESUME STATE verbatim; the previously recorded measurement was 150145 bytes of which 799 was live, so the defect is smaller but unfixed and still unusable. This is the failure `resume-state-currency-signal` exists for, gated behind `Q-58`. (3) `next` WITH NO `--source` reports "no plan source" and "no plan steps found" and exits calmly, rather than discovering the plan or failing loudly, which is false-green-adjacent and rhymes with `Q-55`. CONSEQUENCE FOR THE VALIDATION WORK: defect (1) adds a constraint candidate that is NOT in the list recorded above, namely that a step's declared `[[step.increment]]` set should cover the increments its own brief commits to, and more defensively that `next` must not assert completion from a converged last increment alone. It also strengthens the case that the release gates were the right call.

THE DOMINANT LESSON OF THIS TASK, established independently in BOTH loops: EVERY FIX PASS THAT AUTHORS PROSE MANUFACTURES THE NEXT ROUND'S FINDING. Step 92 rounds 1 and 2 were authoring passes (47 lines added, then 68 rewritten) and both re-seeded; round 3 was constrained to DELETION-ONLY and manufactured NONE. Step 93 rounds 1 and 2 wrote MORE than the verdict prescribed and all the surplus broke; round 3 was constrained to exactly two deletions with a mechanically checkable diff shape (two files, two hunks) and an explicit escape valve ("if you see something else wrong, REPORT it, do not fix it"), and the planner used the valve twice. GOING FORWARD: constrain fix passes to deletions and single-token reverts where possible, give the writer a mechanically checkable diff shape, and always give it somewhere to put the improvement impulse other than the file.

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECTS THIS SESSION, recorded so they are not repeated. (1) I imposed a "property 4" invariant banning the words every/only/authoritative/exhaustive/all outside the COVERAGE block. That word list cannot distinguish a QUANTIFIER from a LOGICAL CONNECTIVE, and it caused a correct one-directional "normalize equal ONLY WHEN" to be rewritten as a false biconditional ("just when"). AMENDED, and the amendment is now live: the rule is SEMANTIC (no COVERAGE CLAIM outside the block); the word list is a CLASSIFICATION AID only and must NEVER trigger a rewrite; logical connectives in mechanical statements are EXEMPT; and the single expected exempt hit (`only when` at the `normalize_wrapping` site) is RECORDED so a reviewer does not read it as a regression. (2) My step-92 consolidation brief FAILED TO CARRY FORWARD a requirement round 2's triage had explicitly set ("narrow this text in the same pass"), which surfaced later as finding `RD-2`. A valid verdict lost in hand-off is exactly what the findings-file rule exists to prevent. (3) Earlier defects already recorded above: risk-class drift, triager worktrees built from the wrong base, and a ledger edit that rotted four line citations.

OUTSTANDING PLAN-SIDE ITEM (planner work, NOT orchestrator or implementer): the step-92 brief `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.steps/prompt-drift-guard.md` still carries the `src/manifest.rs` mis-citation that finding `RD-3` corrected in the code (the `checks-reviewer` row and its `module = "checks"` tag are data in `pack/pack.toml`, around `:219-223`). `optional-modules` increment 2 SUB-INCREMENT 2c-i (the `checks` subcommand with worktree-isolated execution) review (CONVERGED, RISKY, 4 rounds; worktree-isolated). Task `optional-modules-inc2ci`, artifact the `agent-scaffold checks` subcommand (`src/checks.rs` + `src/main.rs` wiring): parses a project's `.agents/checks.toml`, runs `lint` (its `command`) and `format` (its `check` command) checks, `test`/`mutation` skipped, inside a SEPARATE TEMPORARY git worktree (the `Q-39` human decision) so the live tree is never mutated. Isolation via `git stash create` (working-tree state, or HEAD when clean) -> `git worktree add --detach` under the system temp dir; a `WorktreeGuard` Drop removes it on every path; a startup self-healing prune reclaims orphans from killed runs. Built by an isolated implementer in worktree `impl/inc2ci-checks-cmd`. Classified RISKY (new command-execution surface + git worktree manipulation + cleanup correctness + runs arbitrary configured commands). Round 1: two reviewers (opus isolation/security-attack `-reviewer-opus.md`, sonnet parser/glob/errors `-reviewer-sonnet.md`) + separate opus triager (`-triage.md`). Core isolation VERIFIED to hold (no critical/high); 11 valid (3 med + 8 low). Must-fix: F-M1 `RunError::Io` exited 1 not the documented 2 (exit-code mapping single-sourced on `exit_code()` + tested); F-M2 SIGKILL orphan-worktree leak (added a startup prune); F-M3a inherited-stdin hang (`Stdio::null()`); F-DOC the "live tree never touched" overclaim BOUNDED (working-tree isolation of a relative-path check, NOT a sandbox); F-L1 absent-config note -> stderr; plus glob/parser lows. Deferred (documented): per-check timeout / daemon-deadlock, `--dir` subdir cwd, the pre-registration temp-dir residual. Both reviewers + triager AFFIRMED keeping the hand-rolled glob (passed stress tests) and the 0/1/2 exit split. Fix `7ea018c`. Round 1 new_valid (11), streak 0. Round 2 (two fresh reviewers): sonnet CLEAN, but opus (attacking the NEW prune) found a valid MEDIUM, the repo-scoped prune deleted a CONCURRENT same-repo run's worktree (it could not tell a live run from a dead orphan), demonstrated empirically; new_valid (1 med), streak 0. ACCEPTED (not dismissed, so no separate triager per precedent). Fix `3f1e247`: gate reclamation on owning-process liveness (the runner temp-dir name embeds the pid; reclaim only when `/proc/{pid}` shows it dead), doc corrected. Round 3 (opus, empirical incl. a 25-run same-repo concurrency stress): CLEAN, streak 1. Round 4 (sonnet, fresh holistic, all four invariants re-verified): CLEAN, streak 2 -> CONVERGED. 149 tests, clippy clean throughout. The risky loop caught real defects (exit code, SIGKILL leak, stdin hang, overclaim, a two-stage concurrency race) before shipping. MERGE: fast-forwarded to `3f1e247` (main unchanged during the step; 149 tests + clippy green after). Rounds logged (records 74-77). `optional-modules` stays IN PROGRESS (2c-ii remains). NEXT: 2c-ii, `--staged` (isolate the index tree) + `--with-precommit-hook` (scaffold a create-if-absent pre-commit hook running `checks --staged`), reusing this isolated runner. Committing the integration with this note, then commit-deleting the `optional-modules-2ci.reviews/` files.

`optional-modules` increment 2 SUB-INCREMENT 2c-ii (`--staged` + the pre-commit hook) review (CONVERGED via HUMAN DECISION at the round cap, RISKY, 5 rounds; worktree-isolated). Task `optional-modules-inc2cii`, artifact: `--staged` (index-tree isolation via `git write-tree` -> `commit-tree` -> the shared worktree runner), the inert `.agents/hooks/pre-commit` asset (delegates to `agent-scaffold checks --staged`; off-PATH SKIP with a note, human/reviewer-endorsed), the `scaffold --with-precommit-hook` create-if-absent install, and a new `executable` manifest field. Built by an isolated implementer in worktree `impl/inc2cii-staged-hook`. The implementer self-caught a LOAD-BEARING bug via the functional smoke (Principle 6): git sets `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_INDEX_FILE` inside a pre-commit hook, which broke `git worktree add` in the exact hook `--staged` exists for; fixed by stripping the git env from every runner call. Classified RISKY (writes into `.git/hooks`, index isolation, the never-clobber guarantee). This artifact was HARD: the adversarial loop found FOUR distinct write-escapes across rounds, each a variant of the same class, and each fixed. Round 1 (opus+sonnet+separate triager): 9 valid (1 med + 8 low, incl. F1 a dangling-symlink write-through via `exists()`-follows-symlink, the missing `executable` doc, the initial-commit staged test gap); sonnet F-3 (delegate `set -eu`) triager-DISPROVED empirically. Fix `6e9359b` (lstat never-clobber, `HookInstall::Skipped` so install errors never fail the scaffold, tests). Round 2 (opus+sonnet): opus found a HIGH, `--with-precommit-hook` into a SUBDIRECTORY of an ancestor repo installed a broken hook into the ANCESTOR's `.git/hooks/` (repo-root guard added, `9ca7ca9`). Round 3 (opus): another HIGH of the same class, a git LINKED WORKTREE escaped (shared hooks in the main repo); the fix generalised the guard to the load-bearing invariant, the RESOLVED hooks dir must be inside the output dir (`ff45a25`), which subsumed the whole class. Round 4 (opus, exhaustive attack across every git layout): the write-escape class CONFIRMED CLOSED; one new LOW (R4-1, a preview/action mismatch for a fresh-init inheriting a global `core.hooksPath` outside the dir). At this point the artifact had run 4 rounds without a clean one (each found progressively less-severe valid issues, high->high->low), reaching the 5-round total-cap. ESCALATED to the human per the cap contract (options + recommendation, ledger as evidence). Human DECISION: fix R4-1, run ONE clean confirming round, accept, matching ceremony to the now-reduced stakes since the dangerous class is closed and independently confirmed. R4-1 fixed with an honest conditional preview label (`e101539`). Round 5 (sonnet, fresh): CLEAN, streak 1. CONVERGED by the human decision (not the default 2 clean rounds for risky). 168 tests, clippy clean throughout. MERGE: fast-forwarded to `e101539` (main unchanged during the step). Rounds logged (records 78-83, incl. the `escalation` record with `human_decision: decision`). This convergence-below-the-risky-streak is the source of `Q-40` (W3 must be taught to accept it before `optional-modules` is marked complete). `optional-modules` increment 2 is now COMPLETE; increment 3 (isolation module) remains, so the Roadmap step stays IN PROGRESS. Committing the integration with this note, then commit-deleting the `optional-modules-2cii.reviews/` files.

Motivation (human point 2): the log could not answer the core calibration question (are the 1-clean-round-for-low-risk / 2-for-risky constants right?) without the classification, and could not tell whether the second reviewer model earns its cost without attribution.
- `Q-26` DECIDED (human, resurfaced post-compaction as promised): the deterministic-checks module uses a declarative `.agents/checks.toml` (`[[check]]` entries: command, kind, format check-mode command; ast-grep + formatter pre-populated; pluggable). Unblocks `optional-modules` increment 2. Folded into `optional-modules`.
- `Q-27` (open) and `Q-28` (open) ADDED to the queue, both design-pass-first at the human's direction: `Q-27` deterministic workflow-invariant enforcement (extend `validate` with a `--workflow` mode cross-referencing plan status <-> ledger rounds <-> metrics, the recommended detection-not-prevention option); `Q-28` workflow-state navigation/query commands (`state`/`next` emitting a slice for token-efficient resume). They likely converge on one queryable-state surface and share the `state-schema` parsers.

NEXT (revised): (1) run the pending review loop on the metrics-fields change (`f57c87f`) and converge it; (2) build `optional-modules` increment 2 (the deterministic-checks module) now that `Q-26` is decided, starting with its config-layout design pass around `.agents/checks.toml`; then increment 3 (isolation module); then `workflow-calibration`. `Q-27`/`Q-28` await their design passes before any build.
